unknown.","__formatted_results_override":"column_name|data_type|is_nullable|description\nprovider|String|1|Payment processor: apple, google-play, or stripe.\npayment_id|String|1|Unique identifier for the individual payment transaction within its provider.\npayment_group_id|String|1|Grouping identifier linking related payments (original store transaction for Apple/Google, payment intent for Stripe).\nproduct_id|String|1|Product or plan identifier from the payment (RevenueCat product ID for stores, null for Stripe).\nclassification|String|1|Payment type classification: initial (first payer payment), renewal (recurring subscription), repeat-purchase (non-recurring or later subscription), subscription-change (Stripe update/threshold charge), or unresolved.\nprovider_billing_classification|String|1|Provider's classification of the payment: initial, renewal, or unresolved.\nprovider_billing_reason|String|1|Stripe-specific billing reason (subscription_create, subscription_cycle, subscription_update, subscription_threshold, or null for non-Stripe).\nrecurring_payment_key|String|1|Identifier for the recurring subscription lineage (original store transaction for auto-renewable subscriptions, subscription ID for Stripe, null for one-time purchases).\nreporting_time|DateTime|1|Timestamp of the payment in America/New_York timezone, sourced from provider-specific reporting clocks.\nreporting_clock|String|1|Provider-specific timestamp source: apple-period-start, revenuecat-start-time-provisional, or stripe-charge-created.\namount|Float|1|Gross payment amount in the original transaction currency before fees and taxes.\ncurrency|String|1|Three-letter ISO currency code of the payment amount.\nmonetary_basis|String|1|Source of the payment amount: apple-reported-price, revenuecat-purchased-amount-provisional, or stripe-integration-provisional.\nvalidation_status|String|1|Provider-level validation of the payment: valid or invalid.\nexternal_user_id|String|1|External account identifier from the payment provider (RevenueCat app user ID for stores, Stripe metadata or customer ID for Stripe).\ncountry|String|1|ISO country code of the payment's billing location (store country for Apple/Google, billing address for Stripe).\nnet_ratio|Float|1|Decimal fraction (0–1) of gross amount retained after store commission and tax or payment processor fees.\npayer_key|String|1|Unique identifier for the payer within their provider (original store transaction for Apple/Google, customer ID for Stripe).\nduration|String|1|ISO 8601 duration string representing the subscription billing interval (e.g., P1M for monthly, P1Y for annual).\nperson_id|String|1|PostHog person UUID linked to the payment's external user identifier, or null if unresolved or conflicted.\nidentity_status|String|1|Resolution status of the external user identifier to a PostHog person: linked (single match), conflict (multiple matches), unknown (no match or missing identifier).\nmarketing_day|Date|1|Date of the payment in America/New_York timezone, used for daily reporting aggregation.\nunits_per_usd|Decimal|1|Exchange rate from the payment's currency to USD as of the marketing day.\nrow_status|String|1|Validation status of the payment row: valid, invalid, missing-reporting-time, or fx-rate-unavailable.\nusd_micros|Float|1|Gross payment amount converted to USD and expressed in millionths of a dollar, or null if conversion unavailable.\nnet_usd_micros|Float|1|Net revenue in USD millionths after applying net_ratio to usd_micros, or null if either component is unavailable.\nmarket|String|1|Reporting market derived from payment country, grouped as US, UK, CA, AU, NZ, IE, Other, or Unknown.\nplan|String|1|Billing frequency of the payment's subscription plan, categorized as monthly, annual, other, or unknown."}

> ```json
> {
>   "command": "call execute-sql {\"query\":\"WITH purchase_events AS (\\n  SELECT\\n    properties.paymentIntentId AS payment_intent_id,\\n    argMax(properties.purchaseSource, timestamp) AS purchase_source,\\n    argMax(properties.activePaywallTest, timestamp) AS paywall_test,\\n    argMax(properties.paywallTestVariant, timestamp) AS paywall_variant\\n  FROM events\\n  WHERE event = 'subscription-purchased'\\n    AND timestamp >= toDateTime('2026-05-26 00:00:00', 'America/New_York')\\n    AND timestamp < toDateTime('2026-10-31 00:00:00', 'America/New_York')\\n    AND notEmpty(properties.paymentIntentId)\\n  GROUP BY payment_intent_id\\n)\\nSELECT\\n  if(isNull(e.payment_intent_id), 'unmatched', 'matched') AS match_status,\\n  count() AS payments,\\n  round(sum(p.usd_micros) / 1000000, 2) AS gross_usd\\nFROM live_payments AS p\\nLEFT JOIN purchase_events AS e ON p.payment_group_id = e.payment_intent_id\\nWHERE p.provider = 'stripe'\\n  AND p.row_status = 'valid'\\n  AND p.marketing_day >= toDate('2026-05-26')\\n  AND p.marketing_day <= toDate('2026-10-30')\\nGROUP BY match_status\",\"context\":\"Validate the join between Stripe payment facts and verified subscription-purchased paymentIntentId events before attributing revenue to app-to-web sources; governed catalog consulted: no match.\"} --json",
>   "context": "Read-only validation of Stripe payment attribution for resume metrics.",
>   "llm_model": "gpt-6.1-sol"
> }
> ```
>
> ```text
> {"query":{"kind":"HogQLQuery","query":"WITH purchase_events AS (\n  SELECT\n    properties.paymentIntentId AS payment_intent_id,\n    argMax(properties.purchaseSource, timestamp) AS purchase_source,\n    argMax(properties.activePaywallTest, timestamp) AS paywall_test,\n    argMax(properties.paywallTestVariant, timestamp) AS paywall_variant\n  FROM events\n  WHERE event = 'subscription-purchased'\n    AND timestamp >= toDateTime('2026-05-26 00:00:00', 'America/New_York')\n    AND timestamp < toDateTime('2026-10-31 00:00:00', 'America/New_York')\n    AND notEmpty(properties.paymentIntentId)\n  GROUP BY payment_intent_id\n)\nSELECT\n  if(isNull(e.payment_intent_id), 'unmatched', 'matched') AS match_status,\n  count() AS payments,\n  round(sum(p.usd_micros) / 1000000, 2) AS gross_usd\nFROM live_payments AS p\nLEFT JOIN purchase_events AS e ON p.payment_group_id = e.payment_intent_id\nWHERE p.provider = 'stripe'\n  AND p.row_status = 'valid'\n  AND p.marketing_day >= toDate('2026-05-26')\n  AND p.marketing_day <= toDate('2026-10-30')\nGROUP BY match_status"},"results":"match_status|payments|gross_usd\nmatched|19024|1452802.63\nunmatched|14666|834409.41","__formatted_results_override":"match_status|payments|gross_usd\nmatched|19024|1452802.63\nunmatched|14666|834409.41"}
> ```

> codex_apps.posthog.exec
>
> ```json
> {
>   "command": "call execute-sql {\"query\":\"WITH purchase_events AS (\\n  SELECT\\n    properties.paymentIntentId AS payment_intent_id,\\n    argMax(properties.purchaseSource, timestamp) AS purchase_source,\\n    argMax(properties.activePaywallTest, timestamp) AS paywall_test,\\n    argMax(properties.paywallTestVariant, timestamp) AS paywall_variant\\n  FROM events\\n  WHERE event = 'subscription-purchased'\\n    AND timestamp >= toDateTime('2026-05-26 00:00:00', 'America/New_York')\\n    AND timestamp < toDateTime('2026-10-31 00:00:00', 'America/New_York')\\n    AND notEmpty(properties.paymentIntentId)\\n  GROUP BY payment_intent_id\\n)\\nSELECT\\n  toStartOfMonth(p.marketing_day) AS month,\\n  multiIf(e.purchase_source IN ('website','web_funnel','builder_funnel'), 'web-originated', empty(e.purchase_source), 'unknown', 'app-originated') AS origin,\\n  count() AS payments,\\n  round(sum(p.usd_micros) / 1000000, 2) AS gross_usd\\nFROM live_payments p\\nINNER JOIN purchase_events e ON p.payment_group_id = e.payment_intent_id\\nWHERE p.provider = 'stripe' AND p.row_status = 'valid'\\n  AND p.marketing_day >= toDate('2026-05-26') AND p.marketing_day <= toDate('2026-10-30')\\nGROUP BY month, origin ORDER BY month, origin\",\"context\":\"Attribute matched Stripe payments and revenue to verified app/web purchase sources and paywall tests; governed catalog consulted: no match.\"} --json",
>   "context": "Read-only Stripe app-to-web impact analysis.",
>   "llm_model": "gpt-6.1-sol"
> }
> ```
>

>
> ```text
> "The data format is as follows:\n<Data type>\n- $event – description text here\n- another_event\n</Data type>\n...\n---\nResults:\n<Numeric>\n- $geoip_latitude – Approximated latitude matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_longitude – Approximated longitude matched to this event's IP address.\n- $screen_height – The height of the user's entire screen (in pixels).\n- $screen_width – The width of the user's entire screen (in pixels).\n- $geoip_accuracy_radius – Accuracy radius of the location matched to this event's IP address (in kilometers).\n</Numeric>\n<String>\n- $geoip_city_name – Name of the city matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_postal_code – Approximated postal code matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_subdivision_1_name – Name of the subdivision matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_subdivision_1_code – Code of the subdivision matched to this event's IP address.\n- $device_name – Name of the device\n- $os_version – The Operating System version.\n- $geoip_time_zone – Timezone matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_country_code – Code of the country matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_country_name – Name of the country matched to this event's IP address.\n- $locale – The locale of the device\n- $app_build – The build number for the app.\n- $app_version – The version of the app.\n- $geoip_continent_code – Code of the continent matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_continent_name – Name of the continent matched to this event's IP address.\n- $device_manufacturer – The manufacturer of the device\n- $os – The operating system of the user.\n- $os_name – The Operating System name.\n- $geoip_subdivision_2_code – Code of the second subdivision matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_subdivision_2_name – Name of the second subdivision matched to this event's IP address.\n- $lib_version – Version of the library used to send the event. Used in combination with Library.\n- $app_name – The name of the app.\n- $app_namespace – The namespace of the app as identified in the app store.\n- $device_type – The type of device that was used.\n- $lib – What library was used to send the event.\n- url – The deep link URL that the app was opened from.\n- $virt_traffic_type – Classification of traffic: Regular, Bot, AI Agent, or Automation.\n- $virt_traffic_category – Detailed traffic category: ai_crawler, ai_search, ai_assistant, search_crawler, seo_crawler, etc. Bots the project defined itself report the category picked in settings.\n- $virt_bot_name – Name of the detected bot or crawler.\n- $virt_bot_operator – Company or organization operating the bot.\n- $virt_mcp_harness – MCP client label resolved from the event's client identity signals. Empty on non-MCP events.\n</String>\n<Boolean>\n- $is_identified – Client-side property set by posthog-js indicating whether the user has been previously identified on the device.\n- $is_emulator – Indicates whether the app is running on an emulator or a physical device\n- $virt_is_bot – Whether the event was generated by a bot, crawler, or automation tool, detected from the user agent, from operator-published bot IP ranges, or from a bot the project defined itself in settings.\n</Boolean>\n\nNOTE: Some event properties follow dynamic naming patterns and will NOT appear in the list above.\nEach name below ends in an id or a key, so construct it yourself when the user's question needs it:\n- `$feature/{flag_key}` (String): the feature flag value for a specific flag"
> ```

Used in combination with Library.\n- $os – The operating system of the user.\n- $os_name – The Operating System name.\n- $app_name – The name of the app.\n- $app_namespace – The namespace of the app as identified in the app store.\n- $device_type – The type of device that was used.\n- $lib – What library was used to send the event.\n- eas/channel\n- eas/update_group_id\n- $virt_traffic_type – Classification of traffic: Regular, Bot, AI Agent, or Automation.\n- $virt_traffic_category – Detailed traffic category: ai_crawler, ai_search, ai_assistant, search_crawler, seo_crawler, etc. Bots the project defined itself report the category picked in settings.\n- $virt_bot_name – Name of the detected bot or crawler.\n- $virt_bot_operator – Company or organization operating the bot.\n- $virt_mcp_harness – MCP client label resolved from the event's client identity signals. > ```text
> "Description of `FIRST_APP_OPENED`: Fires once when the app is opened for the first time. Tracked via trackAmplitudeOnce (one-time event). Source: app/services/analytics/analytics.ts\n\nThe data format is as follows:\n<Data type>\n- $event – description text here\n- another_event\n</Data type>\n...\n---\nResults:\n<Numeric>\n- $geoip_latitude – Approximated latitude matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_longitude – Approximated longitude matched to this event's IP address.\n- $screen_height – The height of the user's entire screen (in pixels).\n- $screen_width – The width of the user's entire screen (in pixels).\n- $geoip_accuracy_radius – Accuracy radius of the location matched to this event's IP address (in kilometers).\n</Numeric>\n<String>\n- $geoip_city_name – Name of the city matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_postal_code – Approximated postal code matched to this event's IP address.\n- $device_name – Name of the device\n- $geoip_subdivision_1_name – Name of the subdivision matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_subdivision_1_code – Code of the subdivision matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_time_zone – Timezone matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_country_code – Code of the country matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_country_name – Name of the country matched to this event's IP address.\n- $os_version – The Operating System version.\n- $locale – The locale of the device\n- $geoip_subdivision_2_code – Code of the second subdivision matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_subdivision_2_name – Name of the second subdivision matched to this event's IP address.\n- $device_manufacturer – The manufacturer of the device\n- $geoip_continent_code – Code of the continent matched to this event's IP address.\n- $geoip_continent_name – Name of the continent matched to this event's IP address.\n- $app_build – The build number for the app.\n- $app_version – The version of the app.\n- $os – The operating system of the user.\n- $os_name – The Operating System name.\n- appLanguage\n- $app_name – The name of the app.\n- $app_namespace – The namespace of the app as identified in the app store.\n- $device_type – The type of device that was used.\n- $lib – What library was used to send the event.\n- $lib_version – Version of the library used to send the event. Used in combination with Library.\n- $virt_traffic_type – Classification of traffic: Regular, Bot, AI Agent, or Automation.\n- $virt_traffic_category – Detailed traffic category: ai_crawler, ai_search, ai_assistant, search_crawler, seo_crawler, etc. Bots the project defined itself report the category picked in settings.\n- $virt_bot_name – Name of the detected bot or crawler.\n- $virt_bot_operator – Company or organization operating the bot.\n- $virt_mcp_harness – MCP client label resolved from the event's client identity signals. Empty on non-MCP events.\n</String>\n<Boolean>\n- $is_emulator – Indicates whether the app is running on an emulator or a physical device\n- $is_identified – Client-side property set by posthog-js indicating whether the user has been previously identified on the device.\n- $virt_is_bot – Whether the event was generated by a bot, crawler, or automation tool, detected from the user agent, from operator-published bot IP ranges, or from a bot the project defined itself in settings.\n</Boolean>\n\nNOTE: Some event properties follow dynamic naming patterns and will NOT appear in the list above.\nEach name below ends in an id or a key, so construct it yourself when the user's question needs it:\n- `$feature/{flag_key}` (String): the feature flag value for a specific flag"

I found several strong, defensible metrics in the `Solid Starts Production` PostHog project. Stripe and RevenueCat data are synced into PostHog, so the revenue figures come from the production payment warehouse rather than estimates.

Data is through October 30, 2026.

## Strongest metrics

| Area | Concrete result |
|---|---:|
| Mobile product scale | 514,219 monthly active users in October, peaking at 552,095 in August |
| Mobile usage | Roughly 1.9 to 2.1 million app initializations per month |
| App-originated Stripe checkout | 16,822 matched payments from 16,713 payers |
| App-originated Stripe revenue | $1.29M gross and $1.20M net from August through October |
| Default dynamic paywall | 14,456 Stripe payments and $1.12M gross revenue |
| September 40% flash sale | 941 Stripe payments and $58.8K gross revenue |
| Hybrid payment stack | 124,918 Stripe and app-store payments totaling $5.95M gross from August through October |
| RevenueCat app-store path | 99,394 payments and $4.18M gross from August through October |

The app-originated Stripe attribution is conservative. It only includes payments matched from PostHog's `subscription-purchased.paymentIntentId` to the Stripe payment warehouse. Unmatched Stripe payments are excluded.

## Onboarding experiment

The three-way onboarding experiment covered 46,137 users:

| Variant | Users | Purchases | Purchase rate |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Control | 15,304 | 534 | 3.49% |
| Onboarding v2 | 15,357 | 563 | 3.67% |
| Onboarding v3 | 15,476 | 603 | 3.90% |

V3 produced:

- 11.7% higher observed purchase conversion than control
- A 0.41 percentage-point absolute increase
- A 96.3% PostHog-reported chance to beat control

One caveat matters. The experiment's metric definitions were corrected during the run, so I would say "observed lift" rather than present the result as an uncontested causal finding. [View the onboarding experiment](https://us.posthog.com/project/367732/experiments/374234).

## Pricing experimentation

The UK pricing experiment has tested 3,177 users:

| Variant | Purchase rate | Trial-start rate |
|---|---:|---:|
| Control | 16.16% | 15.15% |
| V2 | 19.04% | 18.36% |
| V3 | 18.70% | 18.14% |

The test variants have shown:

- 15.7% to 17.8% higher observed purchase rates
- 19.7% to 21.2% higher observed trial-start rates

This experiment is still running, and PostHog reports a bias-risk finding. Treat these as directional results, not a finished résumé claim. [View the pricing experiment](https://us.posthog.com/project/367732/experiments/386430).

## Other potentially useful results

Use these only if you owned the corresponding feature:

- A choking-quiz experiment reached 474,131 users and increased measured engagement by 32.9%, with PostHog reporting near-100% probability of improvement. [Experiment](https://us.posthog.com/project/367732/experiments/390028).
- A desktop website CTA experiment reached 69,323 visitors and increased header CTA clicks from 0.12% to 0.34%, a 2.82× increase. [Experiment](https://us.posthog.com/project/367732/experiments/462629).
- An A/A instrumentation test processed 46,676 exposures with 99.87% to 99.91% diagnostic completion and no sample-ratio mismatch. [Experiment](https://us.posthog.com/project/367732/experiments/464881).

## Best résumé bullets

- Built a hybrid Stripe and RevenueCat monetization platform supporting 124,918 subscription payments and $5.95M in gross revenue over three months.
- Developed an app-to-web Stripe checkout system that processed 16,822 app-originated payments totaling $1.29M in gross revenue, while preserving native App Store and Google Play purchase paths.
- Shipped a dynamic paywall platform that processed 14,456 Stripe payments and $1.12M in gross revenue, including a promotional flow that handled 941 purchases and $58.8K in gross revenue.
- Designed and ran a three-way onboarding experiment across 46,137 users; the v3 flow increased observed subscription purchase conversion by 11.7% relative to control.
- Rebuilt core mobile experiences for a consumer application serving more than 500,000 monthly active users and handling roughly 2 million app initializations per month.

The revenue analysis uses PostHog's synced payment data and managed-view approach, described in its [revenue analytics documentation](https://posthog.com/docs/revenue-analytics/managed-views). The underlying company views are source-controlled and validation-aware, but they are not marked as certified in PostHog's data catalog.
